Smooth objective F(h) = (1/alpha) log ∑ exp(alpha f_k) over a set of lags S (maybe all). Then gradient = ∑ w_k grad f_k, where w_k = exp(alpha f_k)/∑. We can compute all f_k via correlate O(n^2) but in C. For n up to maybe 1000, correlate O(1e6) okay. But calls many times; gradient requires weights and sum of weighted gradients. We can compute grad of max exactly at sta? For smooth, we can use softmax gradient. Need compute weighted sum of lag gradients. Naively for each active lag m, add grad vector O(n * |S|) potentially O(n^2) Python loops if many lags. But we can compute weighted gradient for all lags via convolution-like operations more efficiently. Let's derive.

For each lag k>0, grad c_k lower (i<k?) Actually k>0: grad for i in [0,n-k-1] lower: -h_{i+k}; for i in [k,n-1] upper: +1 - h_{i-k}. So for k>0 with weight w_k:

def project_sum_box(h,n):
  x=np.clip(h,0,1)
  target=n/2
  for _ in range(100):
    diff=target-x.sum()
    if abs(diff)<1e-15: break
    if diff>0:
       free = x < 1 - 1e-12
       if not free.any(): break
       step = diff / free.sum()
       # but increasing may exceed 1; use max allowed
       inc = np.minimum(1 - x[free], step)?? Need if step small; if step > max room, saturate some.
```

Implement:
```
def project_box_sum(y, n):
    target = n/2
    lo = y.min() - 1.0
    hi = y.max() + 1.0
    for _ in range(80):
       mid=(lo+hi)/2
       x=np.clip(y-mid,0,1)
       s=x.sum()
       if s > target: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
    x=np.clip(y-(lo+hi)/2,0,1)
    # fix residual exactly by distributing among non-bound
    diff=target-x.sum()
    if abs(diff)>1e-12:
       free = (x>1e-14)&(x<1-1e-14)
       if free.any(): x[free]+=diff/free.sum()
       else: ...
       x=np.clip(x,0,1)
       diff=target-x.sum(); if ... set last free? 
    return x
```

Let's think about exploiting one-coordinate convexity for local search. For fixed other variables, objective is max of affine functions in h_i, convex. With sum constraint not fixed? Fix all coordinates except i,j. Let variable for each lag l: f_l(h_i,h_j) = A + b_i h_i + b_j h_j + q h_i h_j, where q is nonzero only if i,j are exactly apart by |l| in either orientation? Actually positive lag term lower? c_{+d}=∑ h_{q+d}(1-h_q) includes pair (i,j) as -h_j h_i? Wait for pair i<j distance d, positive lag at i? term h_j(1-h_i)=h_j - h_i h_j; negative lag at j? h_i(1-h_j)=h_i - h_i h_j. So only two lags involve pair i,j quadratically, with coefficients -1, plus linear. All other lags linear in h_i/h_j. Thus for a move between i and j, only up to two quadratics (bilinear) with coefficient -1 for product h_i h_j (in each of two directed lags), maximum over many linear functions and two quadratics. ```
def grad_sparse(h, n, lags, weights):
  g=np.zeros(n)
  for lag, w in zip(lags, weights):
    if w ==0 continue
    if lag==0: g += w*(1-2h)
    else: m=abs(lag)
      if lag>0:
        g[:n-m] -= w*h[m:]
        g[m:] += w*(1-h[:n-m])
      else:
        g[:n-m] += w*(1-h[m:])
        g[m:] -= h[:n-m]
  return g
```
This matches previous for positive/negative. Need ensure matches c gradient mapping. We derived c_k positive: h_i with lower index? Let's test with n=3, h=[a,b,c], positive lag +1 (index center+1): c1 = h_{1}(1-h0)+ h_{2}(1-h1) = b(1-a)+c(1-b). Derivative: da: -b; db: 1-2b? Wait derivative wrt b: from first 1-a; from second -? c term c(1-b), derivative -c. Total 1-a-c. dc:1-b. Our formula for lag>0: g[:n-m] -= h[m:] => g0-=b, g1-=c; g[m:] +=1-h[:n-m] => g1 +=1-a, g2 +=1-b. Sum g=[-b,1-a-c,1-b]. Good.
Negative lag -1: c_{-1}= h0(1-h1)+h1(1-h2). Derivative: a:1-b; b: -a +1-c; c:-b. Formula lag<0: g[:n-m]+=1-h[m:] => g0+=1-b,g1+=1-c; g[m:] -=h[:n-m] => g1-=a,g2-=b. Sum [1-b,1-c-a,-b]. Good.
Thus direct gradient correct.

Convolution gradient should produce same for full weights. We can test mentally with single positive lag m. For n=3 m=1 w_plus: s[1]=1. left=conv[:3]=[0,a,b]? `project_box_sum(y,n)` with binary search:
```
def project_box_sum(y, n):
    target = 0.5*n
    y=np.asarray(y,float)
    # Quick if already near
    s=np.sum(y)
    if s>0 and s<n:
       x=np.clip(y,0,1)
       diff=target-x.sum()
       if abs(diff)<1e-15:
          return _make_sum_exact(x,n)
    lo = float(np.min(y)) - 1.0
    hi = float(np.max(y)) + 1.0
    for _ in range(100):
       mid = 0.5*(lo+hi)
       x = np.clip(y - mid, 0.0, 1.0)
       ss = x.sum()
       if ss > target:
          lo = mid
       else:
          hi = mid
    x = np.clip(y - 0.5*(lo+hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    return _make_sum_exact(x,n)
```
But if y values are all 0 and target>0, lo=-1 hi=1; binary works. If y large? okay.
`_make_sum_exact`:
```
def _make_sum_exact(h,n):
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    target=0.5*n
    total=h.sum()
    if abs(total-target)<1e-14: return h
    # choose index with value closest to target/n? Let's use median index
    # To minimize adjustment, choose i with room.
    for _ in range(3):
        total=h.sum(); diff=target-total
        if abs(diff)<1e-15: break
        if diff>0:
            # add to interior with smallest value? Actually choose i where h_i < 1 and not close;
            free = np.where((h > 1e-15) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-15))[0]
            if len(free)==0:
                 # add to any <1
                 free=np.where(h < 1.0 - 1e-15)[0]
        else:
            free = np.where((h > 1e-15) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-15))[0]
            if len(free)==0:
                 free=np.where(h > 1e-15)[0]
        if len(free)==0: break
        # distribute diff equally among many to keep values interior
        per=diff/len(free)
        # if per within room? apply min/max; repeat
        h[free] = np.clip(h[free] + per, 0.0, 1.0)
    # choose one interior exact
    total=h.sum()
    # maybe adjust an interior index to target-total_others if possible
    # choose interior with largest slack
    for i in np.argsort(np.abs(h - 0.5)):
       new = target - (h.sum() - h[i])
       if 0.0 <= new <= 1.0:
          h[i]=new
          break
    return h
```
This may leave sum not exactly target due floating, but okay.

But binary search 100 per project in PGD too slow. Use fast project for PGD:
```
def project_feasible_fast(y,n):
    x=np.clip(y,0,1)
    target=0.5*n
    diff=target-x.sum()
    for _ in range(20):
       if abs(diff)<1e-13: break
       if diff>0:
          free = x < 1.0 - 1e-12
          cnt=free.sum()
          if cnt==0: break
          add=diff/cnt
          # if add > max room? cap? Project accordingly by adding add and clip
          x[free] = np.minimum(1.0, x[free] + add)
       else:
          free = x > 1e-12
          cnt=free.sum()
          if cnt==0: break
          sub=-diff/cnt
          x[free] = np.maximum(0.0, x[free] - sub)
       diff=target-x.sum()
    # small exact adjustment among free interior
    if abs(diff)>1e-14:
       free=(x>1e-12)&(x<1-1e-12)
       if free.any():
          # adjust all free equally maybe clip and residual to one
          x[free] = np.clip(x[free] + diff/free.sum(),0,1)
    return _make_sum_exact(x,n)
```
This is faster but binary may be needed if far. We can call full project for initial/resampling, fast in loop.

Now smooth functions:
```
def smooth_value(c_vals, alpha):
   m=np.max(c_vals)
   shifted=alpha*(c_vals-m)
   mx=shifted.max()
   ex=np.exp(shifted-mx)
   z=ex.sum()
   return float(m + (mx + np.log(z))/alpha), ex/z
```

`gradient_full_smooth(h,n,weights)` where weights full length. Could assume sum 1? If active softmax over selected, full weights sum 1. Use dense conv.
```
def gradient_full_weights(h,n,weights):
    dx=2.0/n
    w0=weights[n-1]
    wp = np.zeros(n); wn=np.zeros(n)
    # lags positive are indices n..2n-2 corresponding lag 1..n-1
    wp[1:] = weights[n:]  # length n-1
    wn[1:] = weights[:n-1][::-1]? Need map negative indices 0..n-2 correspond lag -(n-1)..-1. For m=1 (index n-2), wn[1]=weights[n-2]. For m=n-1 (index 0), wn[n-1]=weights[0]. So wn[1:] = weights[:n-1][::-1]
    g = w0*(1-2h)
    if np.any(wn): prefix? Let's compute prefix/suffix.
    # upper_const_i = sum_{m=1}^i wp[m]
    cum_wp = np.cumsum(wp)
    upper_const = cum_wp  # since wp[0]=0, cumsum gives sum 1..i
    # lower_const_i = sum_{m=1}^{n-i-1} wn[m]
    # suffix of wn starting at i+1? For i=0 sum m=1..n-1; i=n-1 sum none
    cum_wn_total = wn.sum()
    # prefix_wn_i = sum_{m=1}^i wn[m]
    lower_const = cum_wn_total - np.cumsum(wn)  # at i, subtract sum 0..i; wp/wn zero at 0, so sum 1..i; lower=total - prefix_i = sum m=i+1..n-1 = sum m=1..n-i-1. Good.
    s = wp + wn
    # left = convolve(h,s,'full')[:n]
    # right = convolve(h,s[::-1],'full')[n-1:2n-1]
    left = np.convolve(h, s, mode='full')[:n]
    right = np.convolve(h, s[::-1], mode='full')[n-1:2*n-1]
    g = g + upper_const + lower_const - (left + right)
    return dx * g
```
Check `right` slice length: n-1 to 2n-2 inclusive length n. `np.convolve` length 2n-1. Good.

Test with single positive lag weights: wp[m]=1, wn=0. upper_const prefix, lower=0, s=wp. Good.
Single negative: wn[m]=1, prefix upper=0, lower suffix. Good.
Lag0 weight: w0.

Sparse gradient:
```
def gradient_sparse(h,n,active,weights):
    dx=2.0/n
    g=np.zeros(n)
    max_idx=n-1
    for idx,w in zip(active,weights):
      if w <= 1e-15: continue
      lag=idx-n+1
      if lag==0:
        g += w*(1-2h)
      else:
        m = abs(lag)
        if lag > 0:
          g[:n-m] -= w*h[m:]
          g[m:] += w*(1-h[:n-m])
        else:
          g[:n-m] += w*(1-h[m:])
          g[m:] -= h[:n-m]
    return dx*g
```
For active length small. Note if active includes index out of range? no.

`active_from_lags(c, K, tol=None)`:
```
def active_from_lags(c,K,tol=1e-9):
   idx=np.argsort(c)[-K:]
   opp = 2*(len(c)-1) - idx
   idx = np.concatenate([idx, opp])
   idx = np.unique(idx)
   # maybe include all near max
   if tol is not None:
      idx = np.union1d(idx, np.where(c >= c.max()-tol)[0])
   return idx
```
For full `None`.

   h=np.array(h,float)

         m=vals.max(); shifted=alpha*(vals-m); mx=shifted.max(); ex=np.exp(shifted-mx); z=ex.sum(); weights=ex/z

                accepted=True; break

Pair/local search:
Previous local_search used full c5 eval per move; could be slower but tiny. We can implement more efficient targeted pair line search:
- Compute all lags c, find worst lag indices. For each worst lag m, consider pairs at distance m: i and i+m. Move mass from higher to lower to reduce that lag and balance. For each pair, evaluate several step values: moving a fraction of difference. But need ensure C5 improves.
- Use analytic quadratic for the active worst lag to find minimizer? For a single lag f_m as function of h_i,h_j. If worst lag positive m= i lower? For pair (i,j=i+m), f_pos = sum other + h_j(1-h_i), f_neg = other + h_i(1-h_j). If we move mass between i and j: h_i -> a, h_j -> b with a+b const. Sum of the two directed lags? Directed pos = const1 + b(1-a), neg = const2 + a(1-b). The max of these two plus other lags. If ignore other lags, objective over a is max(A + b - ab, B + a - ab) with a+b=S. b=S-a. pos = A+S-a - a(S-a)=A+S-a-aS+a^2; neg=B+a -a(S-a)=B+a-aS+a^2. The common quadratic a^2 - a(1+S)? derivative 2a - (1+S). Max of two concave? Actually a^2 - a(1+S) is convex (coefficient +1) because product term?    h=project_box_sum(h_start,n); c=...

` lbfbsb_safe`:
```
def lbfbsb_safe(h,n,alpha,active,rho,maxiter,deadline):
   h=project_box_sum(h,n); c0=compute_c5(h,n)
   # set callback deadline maybe min(deadline-2, time+200)
   try:
      bounds=[(0,1)]*n
      res=minimize(obj_lbfgs, h, args=(n,active,alpha,rho), method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, jac=grad_lbfgs, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-15,'maxls':50,'disp':False})
   except Exception:
      return h,c0
   h_new=project_box_sum(res.x if res.x is not None else h, n)
   c_new=compute_c5(h_new,n)
   if c_new < c0 - 1e-14:
      return h_new,c_new
   return h,c0
```

def cut_lp_step(h,n,active,trust):
   c=compute_all_lags(h,n)
   x=cp.Variable(n); t=cp.Variable()

def projected_gradient_polish(h,n,deadline,active_K=30,alphas=(100,1000,10000),step0=0.1,max_iter=2000):
    h = project_box_sum(h,n)
    best_h = h.copy(); best_c=compute_c5(h,n)
    for alpha in alphas:
        if time.time() > deadline-2: break
        h_curr = h.copy() if h is best? Use current best_h? To avoid degrading, start from best_h.
        step = step0 / max(1.0, np.sqrt(alpha/100))? maybe 0.1 for low, 0.02 high.
        active = None initially; count=0; no_improve=0
        for it in range(max_iter):
            if time.time() > deadline-1: break
            c = compute_all_lags(h_curr,n)
            cur_c = float(np.max(c))
            if active is None or it % 25 == 0:
                active = active_from_lags(c, active_K)
            vals = c[active]
            # if vals has length? unique
            m = vals.max(); sh = alpha*(vals-m); mx = sh.max(); ex=np.exp(sh-mx); z=ex.sum(); weights=ex/z; F=m+(mx+np.log(z))/alpha
            g = gradient_active(h_curr,n,active,weights)
            gn = np.linalg.norm(g)
            if gn < 1e-12:
                break
            # backtracking armijo on F using projected cand
            accepted = False
            t = step
            # maybe if active full dense; for active small direct
            for ls in range(20):
                cand = project_fast(h_curr - t*g, n)
                # if projection barely changes?
                # compute F2 for cand
                c2 = compute_all_lags(cand,n)
                vals2 = c2[active]
                m2=vals2.max(); sh2=alpha*(vals2-m2); mx2=sh2.max(); ex2=np.exp(sh2-mx2); z2=ex2.sum(); F2=m2+(mx2+np.log(z2))/alpha
                # armijo

                if F2 <= F + 1e-4 * t * dot:  # dot negative
                    accepted=True; break
                t *= 0.5
                if t < 1e-7: break
            if not accepted:
                # try accept if full c improves slightly? maybe no
                no_improve += 1
                step *= 0.5
                if no_improve > 30 or step < 1e-8: break
                continue
            h_curr = cand
            # if full c improves best
            c5 = float(np.max(compute_all_lags(h_curr,n))) # we have c2 if accepted, but c2 computed inside; save
```

Function `compute_all_lags`:
```
def compute_all_lags(h,n):
    return np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full') * (2.0 / float(n))
```

`compute_c5`:
```
def compute_c5(h,n):
    return float(np.max(compute_all_lags(h,n)))
```

Projection:
```
def _exact_sum_adjust(h,n):
    h=np.clip(np.asarray(h,float),0,1)
    target=0.5*n
    total=h.sum()
    if abs(total-target) < 1e-14: return h
    # Try choose interior index with minimal adjustment?
    # For all indices compute new = target - (total - h[i]) = target - total + h[i]
    delta = target - total
    # Need h_i+delta in [0,1], choose interior one closest to 0.5.
    candidates = (h + delta)
    # But if h+delta within [0,1], setting that one to h+delta changes total +delta. Wait target-total = delta. If set h[i] += delta, yes new=h[i]+delta.
    # So choose i with 0<=h[i]+delta<=1. There must be if target in [0,n] and current within box? Not always if all at bounds but target between.
    ok = (candidates >= 0.0) & (candidates <= 1.0)
    if np.any(ok):
       # choose index with largest margin? to keep interior
       dist = np.abs(h - 0.5)
       # For ok, pick min dist? Actually choose one with most room? Use min dist to 0.5.
       idxs = np.where(ok)[0]
       idxs = idxs[np.argmin(dist[idxs])]
       h[idxs] = candidates[idxs]
       return np.clip(h,0,1)
    # distribute over a few
    for _ in range(3):
       total=h.sum(); delta=target-total
       if abs(delta)<1e-15: break
       if delta>0:
          free=np.where(h < 1.0-1e-12)[0]
       else:
          free=np.where(h > 1e-12)[0]
       if len(free)==0: break
       per=delta/len(free)
       h[free]=np.clip(h[free]+per,0,1)
    return h
```
This single index adjustment is good: since delta small, find interior that can accept. If delta large (after binary maybe small), fallback.

`project_box_sum`:
```
def project_box_sum(y,n):
    y=np.asarray(y,float)
    target=0.5*n
    # Quick if already
    x=np.clip(y,0,1)
    if abs(x.sum()-target) < 1e-13:
       return _exact_sum_adjust(x,n)
    lo=float(np.min(y)-1.0); hi=float(np.max(y)+1.0)
    for _ in range(80):
       mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
       x=np.clip(y-mid,0,1)
       s=x.sum()
       if s > target: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
    x=np.clip(y-0.5*(lo+hi),0,1)
    return _exact_sum_adjust(x,n)
```
Binary search: Need if `np.min` on empty? n>0. If y all NaN? no.

`project_fast`:
```
def project_fast(y,n):
    x=np.clip(np.asarray(y,float),0,1)
    target=0.5*n
    diff=target-x.sum()
    for _ in range(10):
       if abs(diff)<1e-13: break
       if diff>0:
          free=x < (1.0 - 1e-12)
          cnt=int(free.sum())
          if cnt==0: break
          add=diff/float(cnt)
          x[free]=np.minimum(1.0, x[free]+add)
       else:
          free=x > 1e-12
          cnt=int(free.sum())
          if cnt==0: break
          sub=-diff/float(cnt)
          x[free]=np.maximum(0.0, x[free]-sub)
       diff=target-x.sum()
    # small adjust one interior without full clip? Could just clip
    if abs(diff)>1e-12:
       # distribute among interior
       free=(x>1e-12)&(x<1-1e-12)
       if free.any():
           x[free] = np.clip(x[free]+diff/float(free.sum()),0,1)
    return x
```
No exact, fine.

`smooth_value_weights(vals, alpha)`:
```
def smooth_value_weights(vals, alpha):
    vals = np.asarray(vals,float)
    m = float(np.max(vals))
    shifted = alpha * (vals - m)
    mx = float(np.max(shifted))
    ex = np.exp(shifted - mx)
    z = float(np.sum(ex))
    if z <= 0: # fallback
        return m, None
    weights = ex / z
    obj = m + (mx + float(np.log(z))) / alpha
    return float(obj), weights
```

`smooth_obj_value(x,n,active,alpha,rho=0.0)`:
```
def smooth_obj_value(x,n,active,alpha,rho=0.0):
    c=compute_all_lags(x,n)
    if active is None:
       vals=c
    else:
       vals=c[active]
    obj,_=smooth_value_weights(vals,alpha)
    if rho != 0.0:
       diff=x.sum()-0.5*n
       obj += rho*diff*diff
    return float(obj)
```

`smooth_grad(x,n,active,alpha,rho=0.0)`:
Need return dx? We'll compute c, weights. For active None, weights full. For active not None, if dense use full weights; else sparse.
```
def smooth_grad(x,n,active,alpha,rho=0.0):
    c=compute_all_lags(x,n)
    if active is None:
       vals=c
       if len(vals) > 0:
          _, weights = smooth_value_weights(vals,alpha)
       g=gradient_full_weights(x,n,weights)
    else:
       vals=c[active]
       _, weights=smooth_value_weights(vals,alpha)
       if len(active) > 25:
          wf=np.zeros(len(c))
          wf[active]=weights
          g=gradient_full_weights(x,n,wf)
       else:
          g=gradient_sparse(x,n,active,weights)
    if rho != 0.0:
       diff=x.sum()-0.5*n
       g += 2.0*rho*diff
    return g
```
What if active is an array length 0? no.

`gradient_full_weights` as above. Need if `weights` may be None? no.
```
def gradient_full_weights(h,n,weights):
    dx=2.0/float(n)
    weights=np.asarray(weights,float)
    w0 = float(weights[n-1]) if len(weights)>n-1 else 0.0
    wp=np.zeros(n); wn=np.zeros(n)
    if len(weights) > n:
        wp[1:] = weights[n:2*n-1]  # length n-1
    if n-1 > 0:
        # negative indices: 0..n-2 correspond m=n-1..1 reversed
        wn[1:] = weights[:n-1][::-1]
    g = w0 * (1.0 - 2.0*h)
    # constants
    # upper_const = cumsum(wp) (wp[0]=0)
    upper_const = np.cumsum(wp)
    wn_total = float(np.sum(wn))
    lower_const = wn_total - np.cumsum(wn)
    s = wp + wn
    if np.any(s):
       left = np.convolve(h, s, mode='full')[:n]
       right = np.convolve(h, s[::-1], mode='full')[n-1:2*n-1]
       g = g + upper_const + lower_const - (left + right)
    else:
       g = g + upper_const + lower_const
    return dx * g
```
Check `wp[1:] = weights[n:2*n-1]`: len `weights[n:]` = n-1, indices n..2n-2. Good. `wn[1:] = weights[:n-1][::-1]`: len n-1. For n=1? not possible maybe; if n=1, target .5 but sample? Probably n large; but code handle? We can if n==1: c? But skip. If n=1, `weights[:0]` ok; but target .5; ignore.

`gradient_sparse` as above.
```
def gradient_sparse(h,n,active,weights):
    dx=2.0/float(n)
    g=np.zeros(n)
    center=n-1
    for idx,w in zip(active,weights):
       if w is None: continue
       if float(w) <= 1e-15: continue
       lag = int(idx) - center
       if lag == 0:
          g += w*(1.0-2.0*h)
       else:
          m=abs(lag)
          if m >= n: continue
          if lag > 0:
             g[:n-m] -= w*h[m:]
             g[m:] += w*(1.0-h[:n-m])
          else:
             g[:n-m] += w*(1.0-h[m:])
             g[m:] -= h[:n-m]
    return dx*g
```

`active_from_lags`:
```
def active_from_lags(c,K,tol=0.0,include_center=True):
    length=len(c); center=length//2
    if K <=0: return np.array([center], dtype=int)
    K = min(K, length)
    idx = np.argsort(c)[-K:]
    if tol > 0:
       idx2 = np.where(c >= c.max() - tol)[0]
       idx = np.concatenate([idx, idx2])
    idx = np.unique(idx)
    # add opposites
    opp = 2*(length-1) - idx
    if include_center:
       idx = np.unique(np.concatenate([idx, opp, [center]]))
    # maybe sort
    return np.sort(idx).astype(int)
```
If K small, active may bigger due opposites. For K=10, active up to 21. Good.

def lbfbsb_safe(h,n,alpha,active,rho,maxiter,deadline,maxcall_s=120):
    global _TIMEOUT_DEADLINE
    h=project_box_sum(h,n); c0=compute_c5(h,n)
    bounds=[]; for i in range(n): bounds.append((0.0,1.0))
    # set timeout: min(deadline-3, time.time()+maxcall_s)
    tlimit = min(deadline - 1.0, time.time() + maxcall_s)
    if tlimit > time.time() + 0.5:
       _TIMEOUT_DEADLINE = tlimit
    try:
       res = minimize(smooth_obj_value, h, args=(n,active,alpha,rho), method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, jac=smooth_grad, callback=_timeout_callback, options={'maxiter':maxiter, 'ftol':1e-15, 'maxls':80, 'disp':False})
    except _OptTimeout:
       return h,c0
    except Exception:
       return h,c0
    finally:
       _TIMEOUT_DEADLINE = None
    if res is None or not hasattr(res,'x') or res.x is None: return h,c0
    h_new=project_box_sum(res.x,n)
    c_new=compute_c5(h_new,n)
    if c_new < c0 - 1e-15:
       return h_new,c_new
    # Maybe result is slightly infeasible; project result even if c worse but sum fixed? no use if improves only.
    return h,c0
```

CVX cut step function:
```
def cut_lp_step(h,n,active,trust,deadline,maxcall_s=30):
   if cp is None: return h.compute...
   c=compute_all_lags(h,n); c0=float(np.max(c))
   xvar=cp.Variable(n); tvar=cp.Variable()
   cons=[cp.sum(xvar)==0.5*n, xvar>=0, xvar<=1, cp.abs(xvar-h)<=trust]
   dx=2.0/float(n)
   for idx in active:
       lag=int(idx)-(n-1)
       grad=single_lag_grad(h,n,lag)
       cons.append(c[idx] + dx*(grad @ (xvar - h)) <= tvar)
   obj = tvar + 1e-6*cp.norm(xvar-h,1)
   prob=cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(obj), cons)
   solvers = [cp.GLOP, cp.ECOS, cp.CBC, cp.GLPK]
   best_status=None
```

          prob.solve(solver=solver)
          if prob.status in ['optimal','optimal_inaccurate'] and xvar.value is not None:
              xval=np.asarray(xvar.value,float)
              xval=project_box_sum(xval,n)
              return xval, compute_c5(xval,n), c0

def optimize_at_n(h_start,n,deadline,max_total_s=None):
    h=project_box_sum(h_start,n)
    c=compute_c5(h,n)
    # L-BFGS full low alpha if n<=900 and time

       if n <= 700:
          # full alpha 50 and 300 maybe

`pair_local_search` should not use `best_h` global. It returns best. Implement with top pairs.
Let's write robust:
```
def pair_local_search(h,n,deadline):
    h=np.asarray(h,float).copy()
    best_c=compute_c5(h,n)
    current_c=best_c
    deltas=[0.15,0.075,0.0375,0.01875,0.009,0.0045]
    top_count=max(4, min(12, len(h)//50))
    pair_count=15
    for d in deltas:
       if time.time() > deadline - 0.5: break
       improved=True
       while improved and time.time() < deadline - 0.5:
           improved=False
           c=compute_all_lags(h,n)
           top_idx=np.argsort(c)[-top_count:]
           for idx in top_idx:
               if time.time() > deadline - 0.5: break
               lag=int(idx)-(n-1)
               if lag==0: continue
               m=abs(lag)
               if m >= n: continue
               if lag > 0:
                   source = h[m:]; dest = h[:n-m]
               else:
                   source = h[:n-m]; dest = h[m:]
               diff = source - dest
               # consider pairs where diff > threshold
               order = np.argsort(diff)[-pair_count:][::-1]
               for pos in order:
                   if time.time() > deadline - 0.5: break
                   i = int(pos)
                   if lag > 0:
                       j = i + m
                       if h[j] - h[i] <= 1e-10: continue
                       base = min(d, 0.5*(h[j]-h[i]), h[j], 1.0-h[i])
                   else:
                       j = i + m
                       if h[i] - h[j] <= 1e-10: continue
                       base = min(d, 0.5*(h[i]-h[j]), h[i], 1.0-h[j])
                   if base <= 0: continue
                   for frac in (1.0, 0.618, 0.25):
                       step = base*frac
                       if step <= 1e-11: continue
                       if lag > 0:
                           old_i=h[i]; old_j=h[j]
                           h[i]=old_i+step; h[j]=old_j-step
                       else:
                           old_i=h[i]; old_j=h[j]
                           h[i]=old_i-step; h[j]=old_j+step
                       c_new=compute_c5(h,n)
                       if c_new < current_c - 1e-14:
                           current_c=c_new
                           if c_new < best_c:
                               best_c=c_new
                           improved=True
                           break
                       else:
                           h[i]=old_i; h[j]=old_j
                   # if break inner due accept, continue pairs? improved true but current_c changed; maybe continue loop? If we break frac but continue pair loop, source/dest order outdated but okay. To speed, if accept, maybe continue to next lag or break? We can `if improved: break`? But many potential; while improved loops again. Could break out of pair loops after acceptance to recompute top. Use flags.
```
Better: On accept, break out of all loops and recompute. Use `accepted_break = True`. But Python nested breaks messy. Could simply after acceptance set `break` inner, then after pair loop if improved `break` for top loop and exit while iteration. Need code.

CVX cut polish:
```
def cut_polish(h,n,deadline,max_rounds=3):
   h=project_box_sum(h,n); c=compute_c5(h,n)
   trust=0.12
   for r in range(max_rounds):
      if time.time()>deadline-1: break
      c_arr=compute_all_lags(h,n)

Could evaluate candidate C5 faster for single pair? Full correlate O(n^2). Could derive update of c for all lags under pair move in O(n) maybe! This is important for local search. If h_i and h_j change, c_lag changes only for lags involving i or j, at most O(n) lags. We can update c array in O(n) and evaluate max, instead of recompute full O(n^2). This can make pair search much faster and perhaps stronger. Let's consider implementing incremental c update for pair move.

Given current h and c = compute_all_lags(h,n). If change two coordinates i (lower) and j (upper = i+m maybe) by ±δ, which c_lags affected? c_k = ∑ h_i (1 - h_{i-k}) (using c_k definition). A change in h_p affects c_k if p is the first index (h_p) with term (1 - h_{p-k}), or p is the second index in a pair: term h_q (1 - h_p) where q = p+k. So for each changed coordinate p, affected lags k such that p in [0,n-1] and q=p±k within [0,n-1]. All k from -p to n-1-p, i.e., O(n). For each lag, update contribution exactly. Could derive O(n) update of entire c array for a pair. Then pair local search can evaluate many candidates cheaply.

Let's derive update if h_i -> a + δ, h_j -> b - δ? Or generic Δ vector. c = correlate(h,1-h). Since c is sum of pairs. We can precompute baseline c and for candidate modification, compute delta_c for affected lags. For a single coordinate change Δ at p, effect on c_k:
c_k = ∑_r h_r - h_r h_{r-k}.
Term involving p as first (r=p): contribution h_p (1-h_{p-k}) => Δ_p (1-h_{p-k}).
Term involving p as second (q-k=p -> q=p+k): term -h_q h_p? Actually h_q(1-h_p) = h_q - h_q h_p. Derivative wrt h_p is -h_q. So change Δ_p * (-h_{p-k}) where q=p+k in range.
Wait if h_p changes, and as second index, product h_q*h_p, derivative -h_q. Yes.
But also linear term? c_k formula includes positive linear h_q (for second as 1-h_p? h_q(1-h_p)=h_q - h_q h_p), no h_p linear. As first: h_p - h_p h_{p-k}, derivative 1 - h_{p-k}.
Thus for Δ_p:
For each lag k such that q=p-k in [0,n-1] (p as first): D_c_k += Δ_p*(1 - h_{p-k})
For each lag k such that q=p+k in [0,n-1] (p as second): D_c_k += Δ_p*(-h_{p+k})
Total Δ_c_k = Δ_i*[(1-h_{i-k}) if valid] + Δ_i*[-h_{i+k}] if valid + Δ_j*... for j.
Since for pair move Δ_i=δ, Δ_j=-δ. We can update c for k in range affected. Range k from -max(i,j) to n-1-min(i,j). O(n). Actually for each p, k ∈ [-p, n-1-p]. Union. For p=i,j, intervals length p+1 + n-p-1 = n. Each length n, union up to 2n. Still O(n).
Then candidate c = baseline c + Δc (for all k; for unaffected 0). Max can be computed efficiently if we know baseline max and updated affected indices. ```
def add_point_delta(out, h, n, p, delta):
  if delta ==0: return
  center=n-1
  k1 = np.arange(p-(n-1), p+1, dtype=int)
  idx1 = k1 + center
  out[idx1] += delta * (1.0 - h[p - k1])
  k2 = np.arange(-p, n-p, dtype=int) # -p to n-1-p inclusive, count n
  idx2 = k2 + center
  out[idx2] += delta * (-h[p + k2])
```

Can we make exact O(n) update including cross term? Yes! Since pair changes two coordinates, the total change for each lag can include cross terms only for lags where the two coordinates pair with each other (distance m or -m). def add_point_delta(out, h, n, p, delta):
    if delta == 0: return
    center = n-1
    # first range k: p-(n-1) .. p
    k1 = np.arange(p - (n - 1), p + 1, dtype=int)
    idx1 = k1 + center
    out[idx1] += delta * (1.0 - h[p - k1])
    # second range k: -p .. n-1-p
    k2 = np.arange(-p, n - p, dtype=int)
    idx2 = k2 + center
    out[idx2] += delta * (-h[p + k2])
```

def add_point_delta(out, h, n, p, delta):
    if delta == 0.0: return
    rev = h[::-1]
    # first
    out[p:p+n] += delta * (1.0 - rev)
    # second
    out[n-1-p:n] += -delta * h

For a move that changes h_i += s, h_j -= s (or vice versa), di=s, dj=-s. We need ensure bounds: s within [0,min(source,1-dest)]. We can evaluate multiple fracs quickly. This makes pair search very effective. Let's design `pair_local_search` fully with incremental.

```
def add_point_delta(out, h, n, p, delta):
    if delta == 0.0: return
    # uses out length 2n-1, h length n
    out[p:p+n] += delta * (1.0 - h[::-1])
    out[n-1-p:2*n-1-p] += -delta * h

def candidate_pair_c(h, n, c, i, j, di, dj):
    out = c.copy()
    add_point_delta(out,h,n,i,di)
    add_point_delta(out,h,n,j,dj)
    prod = di*dj
    if prod != 0.0:
       center = n-1
       out[center + (j-i)] -= prod
       out[center + (i-j)] -= prod
    return out
```
Check if `out[center + (j-i)]` and `center+(i-j)` maybe same if i=j; not. Good.

Now pair moves:_For lag > 0, positive c index = center+m. To reduce it, move from upper j=i+m to lower i: di=+s (lower increases), dj=-s (upper decreases). So function `make_move_step(i,j,s, direction)` where direction 'upper_to_lower'.
For lag < 0, negative c index = center-m. To reduce it, move from lower i to upper j: di=-s, dj=+s.
We can find candidate pairs based on current c. For each top lag, choose pairs where relevant difference large. But with incremental, we can afford scanning all pairs for top lags? For n=600, top lags 6, pairs 600 each =>3600, candidate c length 1200 copy -> 4.3M ops per sweep, fine. Try 2 fractions ->8.6M; okay. We can scan all pairs per top lag for each diff>0, evaluate one best fraction maybe. This could improve.

Algorithm:
```
def pair_local_search(h,n,deadline):
  h=np.asarray(h,float).copy(); c=compute_all_lags(h,n); best_c=float(np.max(c)); current_c=best_c
  deltas=[0.15,0.075,0.0375,0.01875,0.009,0.0045,0.002]
  top_lags = max(3, min(10, len(c)//60))
  for d in deltas:
    improved=True
    while improved and time.time() < deadline-0.5:
      improved=False
      c_arr=compute_all_lags(h,n) # to reset exact
      current_c=float(np.max(c_arr))
      top_idx=np.argsort(c_arr)[-top_lags:]
      # Build candidate moves list maybe evaluate on fly; use dynamic threshold.
      # We need evaluate candidate C5 = max(c after point deltas + cross)
      # Use current c_arr.
      # To avoid too many, for each top lag, scan pairs and pick best candidate (lowest max) then commit if improves? Or commit first that improves? For nonconvex, best candidate choice better.
      for idx in top_idx:
         lag=idx-center; if lag==0 continue; m=abs(lag)
         if lag >0:
            # positive: source upper h[m:], dest lower h[:n-m]
            diffs = h[m:] - h[:n-m]
            # only positive diffs > 1e-12
            # Consider all pairs with diff>0 maybe, but only top positions? Could find max c candidate.
            # We'll evaluate candidates for pairs with largest diff, but candidate max also depends other lags. Try top 20 by diff plus maybe random? Use top 30.
            order = np.argsort(diffs)[-min(50,n-m):]
            for pos in order:
               i=int(pos); j=i+m; diff=h[j]-h[i]
               if diff <= 1e-12: continue
               base = min(d, 0.5*diff, h[j], 1.0-h[i])
               if base<=1e-10: continue
               # try fractions: 0.5, 1.0, maybe 0.25
               best_step=None; best_cand_c=current_c+1; best_out=None
               for frac in (1.0,0.5,0.25):
                   s=base*frac
                   if s<=1e-12: continue
                   out = candidate_pair_c(h,n,c_arr,i,j,s,-s) # lower +s upper -s
                   val=float(np.max(out))
                   if val < best_cand_c:
                       best_cand_c=val; best_step=s; best_out=out
               if best_step is not None and best_cand_c < current_c - 1e-13:
                   # commit
                   h[i]+=best_step; h[j]-=best_step
                   c_arr=best_out
                   current_c=best_cand_c
                   if current_c < best_c: best_c=current_c
                   improved=True
                   # after commit, break out? Since c changed, current c_arr updated, but other top lags based old; okay maybe restart while for consistency.
                   # break loops to re-evaluate top lags
                   # We'll break using flag
          else lag<0 similar: move lower to upper: di=-s, dj=+s, diffs=h[:n-m]-h[m:]
      # after all lags, if improved restart while to recompute top? We updated c within loop, but top_idx stale; better break loops on first improvement.
```
If we break on first improvement per sweep, while recomputes c and top. Simpler and safe. Implement `improved` and use `break` with multiple loops. Could use `do ...` but in Python, set `found=True` and break inner, then outer `if improved: break`.

For each top lag, if found, break out of lags and start next sweep. This may be slower (one improvement per sweep) but each sweep O(top_lags*30*candidate O(n)). def golden_pair_step(h,n,c,current_c,i,j,base,sign):
    # minimize max(c_candidate(s)) over s in [0, base]
    gr = (0.5*(5**0.5)-1) maybe predefine

    if b <= 0: return 0, current_c, None
    if b < 1e-12: ...
    x1 = b - gr*(b-a)? Standard: x1 = a + (1-gr)*(b-a), x2 = a + gr*(b-a)
    f1=eval; f2=eval
    for _ in range(18):
       if f1 < f2: b=x2; x2=x1; f2=f1; x1=a+(1-gr)*(b-a); f1=eval
       else: a=x1; x1=x2; f1=f2; x2=a+gr*(b-a); f2=eval
    s = (a+b)/2; f=eval
    return s,f,out
```

Implementation with incremental and simple fractions:
```
def pair_local_search(h,n,deadline):
  h=...
  best_c=compute_c5
  current_c=best_c
  c_arr=compute_all_lags(h,n)
  deltas=[0.15,0.075,0.0375,0.01875,0.009,0.0045,0.002,0.001]
  top_lags = max(3, min(10, len(c_arr)//50))
  pair_count = 40
  fracs = (1.0, 0.618, 0.382, 0.15)
  for d in deltas:
      improved = True
      while improved and time.time() < deadline - 0.5:
          improved=False
          c_arr = compute_all_lags(h,n)
          current_c = float(np.max(c_arr))
          top_idx = np.argsort(c_arr)[-top_lags:]
          center=n-1
          for idx in top_idx:
             lag=int(idx)-center
             if lag==0: continue
             m=abs(lag)
             if lag > 0:
                 diffs = h[m:] - h[:n-m]
             else:
                 diffs = h[:n-m] - h[m:]
             # only consider positive diffs
             pos = diffs > 1e-11
             if not np.any(pos): continue
             # top pair_count by diff
             order = np.where(pos)[0]
             if len(order) > pair_count:
                 # argsort diffs only on pos? Use full argsort
                 order = np.argsort(diffs)[-pair_count:]
                 # filter pos
                 order = [p for p in reversed(order) if diffs[p] > 1e-11]? This is Python list.
             else:
                 order = order[::?]. Maybe simpler: `order = np.argsort(diffs)[-pair_count:]` then loop reversed and skip if nonpos.
             for pos_i in order[::-1]:
                i=int(pos_i); j=i+m
                diff = h[source?]-h[dest?]
                if diff <= 1e-12: continue
                if lag >0:
                    base = min(d, 0.5*diff, h[j], 1.0 - h[i])
                    sign=+1 # lower i +s, upper j -s
                else:
                    base = min(d, 0.5*diff, h[i], 1.0 - h[j])
                    sign=-1 # lower i -s, upper j +s
                if base <= 1e-11: continue
                best_s=0; best_val=current_c+1; best_out=None
                for frac in fracs:
                   s=base*frac
                   if s<=1e-12: continue
                   out = candidate_pair_c(h,n,c_arr,i,j,sign*s,-sign*s)
                   val=float(np.max(out))
                   if val < best_val:
                       best_val=val; best_s=s; best_out=out
                if best_s>0 and best_val < current_c - 1e-13:
                   h[i]+=sign*best_s; h[j]-=sign*best_s
                   # c_arr=best_out optional
                   current_c=best_val
                   if current_c < best_c: best_c=current_c
                   improved=True
                   break
             if improved: break
          # end for
      # end while
  h=project_fast? Pair moves preserve sum and bounds (base ensures), but due floating small; project final exact outside.
  return h,best_c
```

Now, one subtlety: c array from `compute_all_lags` includes dx factor. Our point derivatives are with respect to h without dx? We used formula for c as `np.correlate(h,1-h) * dx`. The derivative of scaled c is dx times derivative of unscaled. Our `add_point_delta` currently uses unscaled contributions (no dx). We need multiply by dx! In candidate_pair_c, if c includes dx, the delta should be scaled by dx. In gradient functions, we multiplied by dx. For pair search c_arr = compute_all_lags scaled. `candidate_pair_c` should output scaled c: old scaled c + dx*(unscaled delta). Thus add_point_delta needs dx or pass dx and multiply. We can incorporate dx by scaling delta before calls: `di_scaled = di * dx`, `dj_scaled = dj*dx`, cross correction `-di*dj*dx`. So in `candidate_pair_c`, compute dx=2/n, call add_point_delta with delta* dx, and cross correction -= dx*di*dj.
Update `candidate_pair_c`:
```
dx=2.0/n
out=c.copy()
add_point_delta(out,h,n,i,di*dx)
add_point_delta(out,h,n,j,dj*dx)
prod=dx*di*dj
if prod: out[center+(j-i)] -= prod; out[center+(i-j)] -= prod
```
`add_point_delta` assumes delta already scaled to c units. Good.

Detailed functions:

`resample_nearest`:
```
def resample_nearest(h, n_old, n_new):
    if n_new == n_old:
        return h.copy()
    if n_old <= 0:
        return np.full(n_new,0.5)
    idx = (np.arange(n_new) * n_old) // n_new
    idx = np.minimum(idx, n_old - 1)
    return h[idx]
```
`resample_interp` as above.

`smooth_value_weights`: if `vals` is an array but large. Use `np.log` double. If alpha very large (1e8), `alpha*(vals-m)` maybe overflow? vals difference up to 1, alpha 20000 safe. We won't use >20000. Good.

`gradient_full_weights`: Need handle len(weights) maybe not 2n-1 if passed full. Ensure if weights length shorter? We'll only pass full. But for safety:

L = len(weights); center=n-1

pos = min(L, 2*n-1)
if pos > n:
    wp[1:] = weights[n:n-1+? pos-n]
```

`cut_polish`:
```
def cut_polish(h,n,deadline,max_rounds=3):
   h=project_box_sum(h,n); current=compute_c5(h,n)
   if cp is None: return h,current
   trust=0.12
   for r in range(max_rounds):
      if time.time() > deadline - 2: break
      c_arr=compute_all_lags(h,n)
      active=active_from_lags(c_arr,12)
      x_new,c_new=cut_lp_step(h,n,active,trust)
      if c_new < current - 1e-13:
          h=x_new; current=c_new; trust*=0.75
      else:
          trust*=0.7
          if trust < 0.01: break
   h=project_box_sum(h,n); return h, compute_c5(h,n)
```

Now, `optimize_at_n`:
```
def optimize_at_n(h_start,n,deadline):
    h=project_box_sum(h_start,n)
    c=compute_c5(h,n)
    # if n tiny, use simple? n>=2.
    if time.time() < deadline - 40:
        # full low L-BFGS
        if n <= 800:
            full_alphas = [(50, 250, 80), (200, 200, 60)] if n<=400 else [(50, 120, 60), (200, 100, 45)]
            for alpha, maxit, call_s in full_alphas:
                if time.time() > deadline - 40: break
                h_new,c_new = lbfbsb_safe(h,n,alpha,None,1000.0,maxit,deadline-10,call_s)
                if c_new < c - 1e-14:
                    h,c=h_new,c_new
    # active L-BFGS
    if time.time() < deadline - 40:
        Ks = [6,15,40]
        for K in Ks:
            if time.time() > deadline - 30: break
            c_arr=compute_all_lags(h,n)
            active=active_from_lags(c_arr,K)
            for alpha in [500,3000]:
                if time.time() > deadline - 25: break
                maxit = 250 if n<=400 else 100
                call_s = 45 if n<=400 else 30
                h_new,c_new = lbfbsb_safe(h,n,alpha,active,1000.0,maxit,deadline-15,call_s)
                if c_new < c - 1e-14:
                    h,c=h_new,c_new
    # CVX cuts
    if cp is not None and n <= 800 and time.time() < deadline - 40:
       sub_deadline = time.time() + min(80, (deadline - time.time())*0.35)
       if sub_deadline < deadline - 20: sub_deadline = deadline - 20
       h,c = cut_polish(h,n, min(sub_deadline, deadline-20))
    # PGD
    if time.time() < deadline - 15:
       sub = time.time() + min(90, (deadline - time.time())*0.35)
       h,c=projected_gradient_polish(h,n,min(sub,deadline-5),K=20,alphas=(200,2000,10000))
    # SLSQP
    if n <= 500 and time.time() < deadline - 15:
       c_arr=compute_all_lags(h,n)
       for K, alpha, maxit in [(5,5000,150), (10,3000,120)]:
          if time.time() > deadline - 8: break
          active=active_from_lags(c_arr,K)
          h_new,c_new=slsqp_active(h,n,active,alpha,maxit)
          if c_new < c - 1e-14:
             h,c=h_new,c_new
          c_arr=...
    # Pair local search
    if time.time() < deadline - 2:
       h,c=pair_local_search(h,n,min(deadline-0.5,time.time()+90))
    h=project_box_sum(h,n)
    c=compute_c5(h,n)
    return h,c
```

Now, `projected_gradient_polish`: Need define constants and ensure no infinite. Let's write carefully.
```
def projected_gradient_polish(h,n,deadline,K=20,alphas=(100,1000,5000)):
    h = project_box_sum(h,n)
    best_h = h.copy(); best_c = compute_c5(h,n)
    for alpha in alphas:
        if time.time() > deadline - 2: break
        h_curr = best_h.copy()
        # maybe if alpha high, step lower
        step = float(np.clip(1.0 / np.sqrt(float(alpha)), 0.01, 0.25))
        active = None
        active_timer = 0
        no_accept = 0
        max_iter = 1500
        for it in range(max_iter):
            if time.time() > deadline - 1: break
            c = compute_all_lags(h_curr,n)
            if active is None or active_timer >= 20:
                active = active_from_lags(c,K)
                active_timer = 0
            else:
                active_timer += 1
            vals = c if active is None else c[active]
            F, weights = smooth_value_weights(vals,alpha)
            if active is None:
                g = gradient_full_weights(h_curr,n,weights)
            else:
                if len(active) > 25:
                    wf = np.zeros(len(c))
                    wf[active] = weights
                    g = gradient_full_weights(h_curr,n,wf)
                else:
                    g = gradient_sparse(h_curr,n,active,weights)
            gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(g))
            if gnorm < 1e-13:
                break
            t = step
            accepted = False
            best_cand = None
            for ls in range(12):
                cand = project_fast(h_curr - t * g, n)
                # if projection moves very little, break
                disp = cand - h_curr
                if float(np.dot(disp, disp)) < 1e-16:
                    break
                c2 = compute_all_lags(cand,n)
                vals2 = c2 if active is None else c2[active]
                F2, _ = smooth_value_weights(vals2, alpha)
                dot = float(np.dot(g, disp))
                # Armijo: F2 <= F + 1e-4 * t * dot. dot should be negative.
                if F2 <= F + 1e-4 * t * dot + 1e-12:
                    accepted = True
                    best_cand = cand
                    c2 = c2
                    break
                t *= 0.5
                if t < 1e-8:
                    break
            if accepted:
                h_curr = best_cand
                c5 = float(np.max(c2))
                if c5 < best_c - 1e-14:
                    best_c = c5
                    best_h = h_curr.copy()
                    # optional reset h_curr to best_h? Let it continue
                step = min(0.5, step * 1.1)
                no_accept = 0
            else:
                no_accept += 1
                step *= 0.5
                if no_accept > 40 or step < 1e-8:
                    break
        # after alpha, if best_h improved, continue; else maybe update h to best_h for next (already)
    return best_h, best_c
```
Potential issue: If active set is fixed and F decreases but best full C5 not improve, h_curr may drift far and become bad. But limited iterations and active updates every 20. Could reset `h_curr = best_h.copy()` after each active update if no C5 improvement? To be safe, in loop after active update, if `compute_c5(h_curr,n) > best_c + 0.001` reset to best_h. But we have c from current. If full C5 h_curr > best_c + 1e-4, reset. Let's add:
```
cur_c5 = float(np.max(c))
if cur_c5 > best_c + 1e-5:
    h_curr = best_h.copy(); step=max(step,0.02); continue
```
At start of each iteration after c computed. This prevents bad drift. But c computed. Good.
Armijo on F can accept moves that do not decrease full c5. If repeated, cur_c5 may increase a bit; reset if too high.

Could use merit F + penalty for full c? Not.

Need `np.clip` step: `step = float(np.clip(1.0/np.sqrt(alpha), 0.01, 0.25))`. For alpha=20000 ->0.007, min 0.01, okay. Maybe high alpha step 0.01 fine.

Now, `smooth_value_weights` if `vals` contains NaN? no.

Potential performance: PGD computes c2 for line search candidates full correlate; if no accept, up to 12 per iter, could be heavy. But step usually accepts after 1-2. Use 8 line search max. We set 10. Good.

Candidate n function `candidate_ns_for`:
```
def candidate_ns_for(base_n):
    prefs=[]
    for mult in (0.65,0.8,0.9,1.0,1.1,1.25,1.5,1.75,2.0):
        prefs.append(int(round(base_n*mult)))
    prefs.extend([120,160,200,240,280,320,360,400,450,500,550,600,700,800,1000])
    out=[]
    for n in prefs:
        if 48 <= n <= 1000 and n not in out:
            out.append(n)
    return out
```
Cap 1000 to avoid too heavy. If parent n 300, includes many. Quick 20 candidates*2=40 evals; n=1000 1M*40=40M, okay. But if time 1100, fine. If budget small, skip.

Function `linprog_cut_step(h,n,active,trust,reg)`:
```
def linprog_cut_step(h,n,active,trust):
   c_arr=compute_all_lags(h,n); dx=2/n; center=n-1
   c_obj=np.zeros(n+1); c_obj[-1]=1.0
   # small random deterministic reg
   idx=np.arange(n)
   c_obj[:-1] += 1e-7*np.sin(idx*0.123+1.23)
   A_ub_rows=[]; b_ub=[]
   for idx_lag in active:
       lag=idx_lag-center
       grad=single_lag_grad(h,n,lag)
       row=dx*grad
       row[-1]=-1.0
       rhs=dx*float(np.dot(grad,h)) - c_arr[idx_lag]
       A_ub_rows.append(row); b_ub.append(rhs)
   A_ub=np.array(A_ub_rows) if rows else None
   A_eq=np.ones((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,-1]=0; b_eq=[0.5*n]
   bounds=[]
   for i in range(n):
       lo=max(0.0, h[i]-trust); up=min(1.0,h[i]+trust)
       bounds.append((lo,up))
   bounds.append((0.0, None))
   res=linprog(c_obj,A_ub,b_ub,A_eq,b_eq,bounds,method='highs')
   if res.success and res.x is not None:
      x=np.asarray(res.x[:n],float)
      x=project_box_sum(x,n)  # may leave trust; okay? If project outside trust, may violate linear cuts. But we only use if improves. Could clip to trust instead? Project may move beyond trust; but feasibility of original not require trust. We can accept if improves.
      return x, compute_c5(x,n)
   return h, compute_c5(h,n)
```
This is a convex LP using linear approximations (not valid but heuristic). It can make larger steps. Need ensure A_ub inequality direction matches linprog: A_ub @ vars <= b_ub. Good.
Rank of A_eq: 1. `linprog` may complain if A_ub None. If active empty, return.
Time: solving LP with 15 constraints and 600 vars using HiGHS very fast. Use several rounds. This is safer than cvxpy. Let's remove cvxpy import? We can still import but not needed. To reduce import overhead, skip cvxpy. The task allowed but not required. Use scipy only. Good.

If linearization overestimates/under, no guarantee. But as heuristic.

def cut_polish(h,n,deadline,max_rounds=4):
   h=project_box_sum(h,n); current=compute_c5(h,n)
   trust=0.12
   for r in range(max_rounds):
      if time.time()>deadline-2: break
      c_arr=compute_all_lags(h,n)
      active=active_from_lags(c_arr,15)
      x_new,c_new=linprog_cut_step(h,n,active,trust)
      if c_new < current - 1e-13:
          h=x_new; current=c_new; trust*=0.8
      else:
          trust*=0.7
          if trust<0.02: break
   h=project_box_sum(h,n); current=compute_c5(h,n)
   return h,current
```
Since LP can project outside trust, but c improves. Good.

```
def project_box_sum(y, n):
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=float)
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    x = np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0)
    if abs(float(x.sum()) - target) < 1e-14:
        return _exact_sum_adjust(x, n)
    lo = float(np.min(y)) - 1.0
    hi = float(np.max(y)) + 1.0
    for _ in range(80):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        x = np.clip(y - mid, 0.0, 1.0)
        s = float(x.sum())
        if s > target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    x = np.clip(y - 0.5 * (lo + hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    return _exact_sum_adjust(x, n)
```
If y length 0, min error. n>0.

`_exact_sum_adjust`:
```
def _exact_sum_adjust(h, n):
    h = np.clip(h,0,1).copy()
    target=0.5*float(n)
    total=float(h.sum())
    delta=target-total
    if abs(delta)<1e-15: return h
    candidates = h + delta
    ok=(candidates>=0.0)&(candidates<=1.0)
    if np.any(ok):
       idxs=np.where(ok)[0]
       # choose index with most interior (min abs(h-0.5))
       dist=np.abs(h - 0.5)
       pos=np.argmin(dist[idxs])
       h[idxs[pos]]=candidates[idxs[pos]]
       return h
    for _ in range(5):
       total=float(h.sum()); delta=target-total
       if abs(delta)<1e-15: break
       if delta>0:
          free=np.where(h < 1.0 - 1e-12)[0]
       else:
          free=np.where(h > 1e-12)[0]
       if len(free)==0: break
       h[free]=np.clip(h[free] + delta / float(len(free)),0,1)
    return h
```
If candidates chosen, sum maybe exact? Good.

`project_fast`:
```
def project_fast(y,n):
    x=np.clip(np.asarray(y,dtype=float),0,1)
    target=0.5*float(n)
    diff=target - float(x.sum())
    for _ in range(12):
       if abs(diff)<1e-13: break
       if diff>0:
          free = x < (1.0 - 1e-12)
          cnt=int(np.count_nonzero(free))
          if cnt==0: break
          x[free] = np.minimum(1.0, x[free] + diff / float(cnt))
       else:
          free = x > 1e-12
          cnt=int(np.count_nonzero(free))
          if cnt==0: break
          x[free] = np.maximum(0.0, x[free] + diff / float(cnt)) # diff negative
       diff = target - float(x.sum())
    return x
```
If diff negative, adding diff/cnt subtracts. Good. No exact.

```
def gradient_full_weights(h,n,weights):
    weights=np.asarray(weights,dtype=float)
    if weights.size != 2*n-1:
       # fallback sparse? But should not.
    center=n-1; dx=2.0/float(n)
    w0=float(weights[center]) if weights.size>center else 0.0
    wp=np.zeros(n); wn=np.zeros(n)
    if weights.size > n:
       wp[1:] = weights[n:min(weights.size,2*n-1)]
    if n-1 > 0 and weights.size >= n-1:
       wn[1:] = weights[:n-1][::-1]
    g = w0*(1.0-2.0*h)
    upper_const = np.cumsum(wp)
    lower_const = np.cumsum(wn)[::-1]
    s = wp + wn
    if np.any(s):
       left = np.convolve(h, s, mode='full')[:n]
       right = np.convolve(h, s[::-1], mode='full')[n-1:2*n-1]
       g = g + upper_const + lower_const - (left + right)
    else:
       g = g + upper_const + lower_const
    return dx * g
```
`np.cumsum(wn)[::-1]` length n. If wn all zero, okay. `np.any(s)` works.

```
def top_indices(values,K):
    K=min(K,len(values))
    if K<=0: return np.array([],dtype=int)
    if K >= len(values): return np.arange(len(values))
    return np.argpartition(values, len(values)-K)[-K:]
```
Top values not sorted. For active_from_lags, we don't need sorted; unique. For pair search, order of top_idx not important. Use this. Need if K >= len. Good.
`np.argpartition(values, len(values)-K)` partition such that last K are largest but unsorted. Good.

`active_from_lags`:
```
def active_from_lags(c,K,tol=0.0,include_center=True):
   length=len(c); center=length//2
   if K<=0 or length==0: return np.array([0])
   idx=top_indices(c,K)
   if tol>0.0:
      idx2=np.where(c >= c.max() - tol)[0]
      idx=np.concatenate([idx,idx2])
   idx=np.unique(idx)
   opp=2*(length-1)-idx
   if include_center:
      idx=np.concatenate([idx,opp,[center]])
   return np.unique(idx).astype(int)
```
`center=length//2` equals n-1 for length 2n-1. Good.

`top_indices` if K is exactly length? For `len(values)-K` =0 if K>=len, but argpartition kth? Need handle. So if K>=len return arange.

`single_lag_grad`:
```
def single_lag_grad(h,n,lag):
    g=np.zeros(n); m=abs(lag)
    if lag==0:
       return 1.0 - 2.0*h
    if m>=n: return g
    if lag>0:
       g[:n-m] -= h[m:]
       g[m:] += 1.0 - h[:n-m]
    else:
       g[:n-m] += 1.0 - h[m:]
       g[m:] -= h[:n-m]
    return g
```

`linprog_cut_step`: Need `A_eq` row length n+1. `c_obj` length n+1. Reg: `reg = 1e-7 * np.sin(0.013*np.arange(n) + 1.234)`. Add to x obj. Objective minimize t + reg·x. Since sum fixed, reg direction biases. Might bias toward smaller x if reg positive/negative? Sum fixed, okay.
If no active rows, return input.
`linprog` may emit warnings? Not. If `res.status` not 0, skip. The default HiGHS may return success. Use try.
```
def linprog_cut_step(h,n,active,trust):
    h=np.asarray(h,float)
    c_arr=compute_all_lags(h,n)
    current=float(np.max(c_arr))
    if len(active)==0: return h,current
    dx=2.0/float(n); center=n-1
    # ensure active within
    A_rows=[]; b_list=[]
    for idx in active:
       idx=int(idx)
       if idx<0 or idx>=len(c_arr): continue
       lag=idx-center
       grad=single_lag_grad(h,n,lag)
       col=dx*grad
       A_rows.append(np.concatenate((col, np.array([-1.0]))))
       b_list.append(float(np.dot(col,h)) - c_arr[idx])
    if not A_rows: return h,current
    A_ub=np.array(A_rows)
    b_ub=np.array(b_list)
    A_eq=np.ones((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,-1]=0.0
    b_eq=np.array([0.5*float(n)])
    bounds=[]
    for i in range(n):
       lo=float(h[i]) - trust
       up=float(h[i]) + trust
       if lo<0.0: lo=0.0
       if up>1.0: up=1.0
       if up<lo: up=lo
       bounds.append((lo,up))
    bounds.append((0.0, None))
    obj=np.zeros(n+1); obj[-1]=1.0
    idxs=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
    obj[:-1] += 1e-7*np.sin(0.0137*idxs + 1.2345)
    try:
       res=linprog(c=obj, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
    except Exception:
       return h,current
    if res is not None and res.success and res.x is not None:
       x=np.asarray(res.x[:n],float)
       x=project_box_sum(x,n)
       c_new=compute_c5(x,n)
       return x,c_new
    return h,current
```

def candidate_ns_for(base_n):

def candidate_pair_c(h,n,c,i,j,di,dj):
    dx=2.0/float(n)
    out=c.copy()
    if di != 0.0: add_point_delta(out,h,n,i,di*dx)
    if dj != 0.0: add_point_delta(out,h,n,j,dj*dx)
    center=n-1
    quad = dx*(di*di + dj*dj)
    if quad != 0.0: out[center] -= quad
    cross = dx*di*dj
    if cross !=0.0:
       out[center + (j-i)] -= cross
       out[center + (i-j)] -= cross
    return out
```

def project_fast(y,n):
    x = np.clip(np.asarray(y,dtype=float),0.0,1.0)
    target=0.5*float(n)
    diff=target - float(x.sum())
    for _ in range(12):
        if abs(diff) < 1e-13:
            break
        if diff > 0.0:
            free = x < (1.0 - 1e-12)
            cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(free))
            if cnt == 0: break
            x[free] = np.minimum(1.0, x[free] + diff / float(cnt))
        else:
            free = x > 1e-12
            cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(free))
            if cnt == 0: break
            x[free] = np.maximum(0.0, x[free] + diff / float(cnt))
        diff = target - float(x.sum())
    return x
```
